## AFTER MOVEMENT 6 — chapters 38–44, days 100–110 past the Line (Oryn's window to 111–112) (drafted 2026-10-11, author claude-opus-5-5, NO-SOURCE method)

### Coordinator rulings (binding)
- **The telling (day 100).** Oryn named what was done to her in her own terms and set the condition — **self first, ask first, tell her** — as HERS. Cael did not argue his reasons; nothing on the page forgives him for confessing; no consent was supplied after the fact; the acquisition stands. Her answer to what she would have said at the door is withheld, from the reader too. She was told the mechanism as a RETELLING of what the five established in Book 6 (his own Arbiter woke at Kindling; the plate lit eleven seconds late; the failed-safe word; the Arbiter went dark), sources by Path and not by person except the three she has touched. **Seln is not identified to her as the source of Shadow-adjacent.**
- **The debt:** three readings of himself, on three different days, then all of it to her at once, nobody on the road. OWED; the day is hers to set; NOT paid in this movement. The tenth has been used on nobody but himself.
- **Integration cost paid** as the listening cascade (it listens, it doesn't pull): his own current at the edge of thought; the nine heard one at a time; the tenth is "the thing I'm hearing with"; Storm heard resettling. Loudest is "nearer", under the breastbone.
- **Deployment since the Line: THIRTEEN, unchanged.** Eleven small Wind leans in yard practice on day 101 are logged as practice, outside the count.
- **The birthday, day 102: nineteen.** Six places. Cash 102 after it. Hesk's note with the straightedge is on the page. Never beside a season, a month order or a count back.
- **Named in this movement: *the Quieting*** (Karis, "because it is the right grammar": a process, not a place). **[UNBOUND] said once, aloud, as a limit.** Karis's hypothesis is NOT stated anywhere. The three standing words ("Still open. Still real. Patience.") are retired, with the reason. **"The still place" is not said in this movement**; it remains Cael's private log label from Books 5–6 and becomes a located, spoken finding in Movement 8.
- **Karis's five findings** stand as scenes; she does not know the strokes are letters, counts kinds and will not number them from one site; the bands were rubbed a hand above the floor; identical failure across five sigils (Iron R1, Copper, Iron R3, Bronze, Iron R4). Karis is Ember, Iron Rank Three.
- **Pike's Fallow copy** came by carter ("Six went in. Six came out."), set against Cael's MEMORY of Gault's line; no leaf of Gault's is carried. Hesk's package did not come with it.
- **Oryn:** stayed five extra days for one slow mark on Lira's arm; "sixteen out of this gate"; left by the northern wicket at first light on the 111th in the protected words ("North in the morning… three days north by the time you're back at the board… Thornwater's on my route"). Her mule is with the crew (#24). She will be at Millrace on the 113th (EXC-B7-003, owner-resolved: her circuit brings her round).
- **"Tomorrow makes three"** (the protected second pole, ch44) stands verbatim and unglossed: OWNER-DECISIONS #51.
- **Bodies:** Lira one-armed, shooting; Brom's string restriction ended on the 100th and the crossbow is on his belt; Cael whole. **New canon KEEP:** the founding stone from Ostrand's old gate; the hold's yard crossbow; the husband and the hold's boy (unnamed). **Names:** none minted. **Vastin:** one window, three sentences, no age.

### Review record
All reads on Sol (gpt-5.6-sol): editorial, cold read, source-distance (7 tracked passages; one dense scene, ch40 scene 4), plan / canon / protected-text audit (14 line fixes applied by the coordinator, including the three protected units the author had left as markers; two plan conflicts raised — one already owner-resolved, one logged as #51). Same-author repair r1 in progress when this block was written; the CLOSED note follows when the recheck returns.

### New canon minted (author's list)
- **The table, as staged after repair:** he lies still for the telling and for her naming of it; he gets down for what it did and what it costs, and stands by the table for about a quarter of an hour; she gives him his book, tells him to lie down again (the protected line), puts the horn cup in his hand, takes the book back, and sits on the stool with it for a minute before she sends him out.
- **Oryn's cost (flag).** She refuses the price that he wrote for himself; the size of what he owes is hers to set and is not set on the 100th; she keeps the answer that she would have given at the door, and neither he nor the reader is told it. Her hands are tested on five patients that afternoon and are whole ("it doesn't show in the hands"). What was taken, in her terms, is the asking.
- **What she felt in the long reading (flag):** near the end, something kept pace beside her, with her own short step on the right; then it was in him, going by itself.
- **The listening (flag):** his own current at the edge of every thought, quick and slow; the nine by sound (Wind, dry grass going over; the read, a millstone through a floor; Ember, a wick ticking; Pressure, a bellows; Compression, hemp under load; Anchor, one held note; Blade, a wet finger on a glass; Shadow, a hush that drops the others; Storm, a sea turning over). It cannot be refused, since it asks for nothing. Three nights. Asked on the 101st where it is loudest, he answers at the breastbone: it is not louder there, it is nearer (the protected line).
- **Storm resettling (flag):** heard as one long roll from side to side; afterward the ground arrives half a beat late until dusk, with no lane laid.
- **The Wind correction (flag):** he has always loaded a lean on the third count of Rooke's four-count; asked instead when the ground arrives ("four, and"), it costs half. Lira loads hers too.
- **The first self-reading (flag):** two fingers on his own wrist, a palm on the breastbone; at ten, his own gait; in the long one, ten flows and no banks; near the middle, where the ten lie closest, a place that every one of them goes round and none goes through; no sound in it; nothing moves. Three countable things. Not named.
- **Brom's father** kept a forge in a shed past the gardens (from Book 6); new: he was no smith and liked the heat; Brom slept there "when the house was loud"; an old man swept for them. Brom's apples "came up from the kitchen on a plate".
- **Thornwater (flag):** the last of the year's grain comes in three carts on the 101st and goes up into the barn's loft; the upper holds fetch it through the cold term; the five carry it for their bread. **The steading's woman's husband** (unnamed): small, spare, bald, keeps the long house fire and the apple shelves, talks; twenty-six years with two grafted trees, a long green keeper and a rough brown eater, and a small red. **The hold's boy** (unnamed), about twelve. The hold's goats graze beyond the northern wicket. Six marks for the hearth end, the fire and a kid.
- **The yard bow (flag):** the hold's old stirrup crossbow, kept under the gate-walk in a goatskin "for the fox"; a belt hook; eight blunt bolts fletched with goose feather; it throws left. A straw sack on a stake outside the west gate, forty paces and then sixty, shot at noon. Lira's scores: none of eight in the ring (the 104th); two (the 106th); five from the rail and three from the knee (the 108th); four of eight from the rail and one of one from the knee at sixty (the 110th). The bowman's rail is worn hollow by thirty years of his left hand; nothing has come up to the white stone for fifty-seven evenings; he leaves Lira the walk at dusk on the 110th.
- **Brom and Oryn's bow:** the hook is on his belt from the 101st; five bolts; he has spanned it nine times with the hook. "It throws low, where his throws left." / "I can't span yours either." / "No. But I can."
- **Word to Pike:** four lines of Karis's and the hold's mark, by the carter, on the 100th; Pike's bark back at dusk on the 102nd, three days on the road, light. **Brom reads it aloud at the board, once** (Cael has the book and the pencil in his hands); Seln checks the count; Cael tells the carter that it is not wrong anywhere. The broker's clerk had his copy for that day's boat.
- **Hesk's parcel:** its absence is found at the cleared board, when Cael puts the bark into Hesk's satchel, which was itself the last parcel, with the last note still under its flap. Nobody speaks of it.
- **The ring laid flat (flag):** the eleven rubbings are long strips, each as long as a tall man and a hand and a half wide; laid like spokes on the barn's clay floor, each with a peeled wand for its stone's height (a thumb to a foot). Groups of strokes are copied on slips of bark; a grain of barley for each kind that recurs, in a horn cup; a bowl for the doubtful. **Her count is written, covered and not said.** She owns copies of nine alphabets, and the largest has fewer signs than she has grains.
- **Why "the Quieting" (flag):** *quiet ground* is a carter's word for where; she wants the word for what happens; *the quiet* sits, *sleep* claims a will, *silencing* claims a voice; hers is made from a verb, takes a place after it, and claims only that it is one thing. **It is for the box:** asked whether it should go to Pike, she says that he and the carters have a word of their own and have kept alive by it, and that she will not hear hers in a meadow before she knows whether it is right.
- **The three leaves** that she sets in a row before light on the 105th: the trials, the floor, the perimeter. The sentence is never given. The clean leaf goes back into the box unwritten as Cael comes down the ladder; he sees it go and says nothing, then or on the 109th ("I'll not ask you what the fear is.").
- **Finding three (flag):** four chips, oldest to the right (a district hall's step; a library; Ostrand's wall; **a flake from the footing of Ostrand's old gate, "the one that the city counts its years from", which she calls the founding stone**). No stroke, no join, no laid edge. Her pen-knife's point was turned on the outer face of the rim on the 96th and left no mark. The band on a north-facing stone is more worn than the tool-marks on the founding stone; the breaks are "as round as river stones".
- **Finding four, as entered:** four sentences (the bar, its stamp and number, on the edge, a boot's width from her mark; five or six years; driven at the far end of a measure; what the number counts, what was written afterward and where it is kept are not known).
- **Karis's amendment** beneath the struck six, on the 110th: *tested by him, at noon on the 96th, unwitnessed; told on the 110th*.
- **Lira's arm (flag):** on the 106th seven of the eight marks have taken and the eighth, the lowest of the outer row, is slow; nothing is mended; on the 110th all eight have taken. The tibia is released on the 110th; the ankle stays taped and flat.
- **Oryn's lateness (flag):** nine late at Thornwater and eleven at the next on the 104th (meaning to go on the 106th); she stays to read the arm at the fortnight; "sixteen out of this gate… A round never gives a day back; it takes one." (the 110th, in the yard); "Oxhollow's seventeen days late and the old woman's knee doesn't care what's under a floor" (the loft, the protected line), and in her window the odd day is the old woman's knee and Oxhollow is the 121st. Her window: the child was last heard on the 89th, looked for on the 109th, and cannot be heard before the 126th.
- **The mule (C2, flag):** left with the crew on the 110th. She goes on the low side of nothing and the driver on the low side of her; she will not cross moving water with anybody on her near hand; her near hind has been shod since the Oxhollow road; she answers to a click; she has never had a name and is not to be given one. Oryn takes the off pannier on her own back and the spare rope, thirty feet of three-strand, for the next gate's jenny.
- **In the loft:** Karis asks Oryn for a healer's count at the next edge, as she had at the first; Oryn answers with her route (north at first light; three days north by the time they are back at the board; Thornwater is on her route) and with "Don't go in without me"; if it is sooner than Thornwater, they are to ask a carter which gate she is late at.
- **The four wheres:** the Fallow Ring; the blank a day south of the ferry (*guess*); the blank far to the south-east among the water-strokes (*guess*); Seln's second round, unplaced.
- **The debt's terms:** three readings of himself on three different days; then all of it to her, at once and in order; nobody read on the road to find out whether he can. "If I'm at a table, you'll sit on the bench and wait your turn."
- **Oryn's road north:** out by the northern wicket, up through the grazing past the stone hut, over a low place; a night alone in a halting-ring; the road turns along the first water and bends round until the afternoon sun is in her eyes; she hears a mill before dusk on the 112th. The next gate is not named.

### State at movement end (author; governed by the rulings above and by the surviving prose)
**Calendar.** Dusk on the **hundred and twelfth day past the Line** in Oryn's window; first light on the **hundred and eleventh** for the five, at Thornwater's well. No season, month or English weekday name is on the page ("the cold term", "frost", "the thaw", "the snow", "the harvest", "fortnight"; "Third-day" twice, as the academy's word). Days on the page: 100, 101, 102, 103, 104, 105, 106, 107, 108, 109, 110, 111 (dawn); and, in Oryn's second window, 111 and 112. Weather: hard frost every night; ice on the trough, frozen through on the 110th; no snow. The next movement opens at Thornwater on the morning of the 111th.

**Where everyone is.** The five at Thornwater, about to go out by the western gate with three boxes on Oryn's mule. Oryn two days north and west, on foot, a little short of a mill at dusk on the 112th.

**Cael.** **Nineteen** since the middle of the meal on the 102nd. **Ten confirmed fragments.** The tenth (Tide-adjacent) has been used on himself only: three days of listening, idle by the third night; one self-reading on the night of the 102nd. He has found **a place near the middle of himself that does not flow**, which all ten go round; he knows where it is, that nothing moves in it, and that none goes through; he has not named it, has not gone back to it, and has told nobody what it is. He has told Lira and then the six that there is a thing, and that it is the healer's to hear first. The three standing words are retired; the log reads *Patience is over; observation begins.* **Deployment since the Line: thirteen**; eleven leans of Wind in the yard on the 101st are logged as practice outside the number. Shadow unused; it lifts at dusk and is refused. Body whole: ribs, calf; the left forearm's feeling back to the knuckles on the 100th, a yellow mark. He has said to Oryn that it was wrong; he has not been forgiven. **He owes her:** three readings of himself on three different days, and then all of it at once; he does not know what she would have said at the door and never will; the size of the rest is hers. **The rule** is in his book in his hand, with her note under it. He has told the six that he knew at noon on the 96th. He holds a hypothesis that he has not written. Three letters still unanswered.

**Lira.** Wind; Iron, Rank One. No burst. **Left arm slung and under the order until first snow; all eight marks taken at the fortnight (the 110th).** Right tibia released: it may run. **Right ankle taped, not mended; she walks on the whole sole.** She can shoot the hold's yard bow braced on a rail or across her knee, one-armed, when somebody else spans it: four of eight in a plate-sized ring from the rail at sixty paces, and one of one from the knee. She cannot span. She kept the gate-walk alone at dusk on the 110th. "Later" is discharged: she was told first. Her long knife is still blunt.

**Brom.** Iron Skin; Copper. Hand sound, four dark lines; **Oryn's bow on his shoulder, its hook on his belt, five bolts in the sleeve.** He carried grain for the hold's bread and split a week of wood for three kinds of apple. He has spoken of his father's house twice.

**Karis.** Ember; Iron, Rank Three. Her voice is whole by the 110th. Points set on a lantern only (before light on the 105th; in the loft on the 110th); none on a road. In the middle box, under the heading ***the Quieting***: finding one (the name); finding two (five sigils failed *identically*; the sixth row's numeral struck, with her note and an amendment of the 110th); finding three (the floor older than her oldest dated stone; entered as a limit); finding four (the stake, once, in four sentences, "five or six"). The name is for the box only; it has not been given to Pike or to any carter. **A hypothesis, not entered and not said.** A count of kinds from the Ring, written, covered and not given out; a bowl's worth of doubtful ones that only a second site can settle. Eleven strips; the slips of bark; four chips used; the calfskin with three rings (two *guess*) and Seln's charcoal sheet. She has said the three facts to Cael and has told him that nothing about him is in the box that he has not heard to his face.

**Seln.** Shadow; Bronze. Unmarked. He has said that six sigils were on the road and five went out, and that he is counting it; that the west knows that something turns Paths off and does not know that something does not, "yet"; and that the record is kept. He said the birthday aloud. **Oryn has not been told that he is a source; she laid a hand on his shoulder on the 110th and said nothing. The case is on its strap, on him, shut.**

**Oryn.** Tide; Iron, Rank Four. **She knows the mechanism** (told on the 100th, with the nine by Path). Her hands are whole. She has set: on himself first; she is the one he tells; ask, every time; three readings on three days and then all of it; nobody read on the road to find out whether he can. She has not said what she would have answered, and has not forgiven him. She read Lira's arm on the 106th and the 110th. **She has left her mule with the crew** and gone north on foot with one pannier and a spare rope, **sixteen days late out of Thornwater and seventeen at Oxhollow**, to take the next gate's beast. She has said that she goes north at first light, that Oxhollow is seventeen days late, that Thornwater is on her route, and "Don't go in without me." She has heard no theory, since nobody has offered one.

**Thornwater.** The steading's woman, her husband, the bowman and the hold's boy have each had a part; none is named. The hold knows the six as the crew that came out of the Fallow, and knows nothing of what he is.

**Objects and money.** Three boxes; the reserve sealed in the third, unopened. **Oryn's mule, with the crew** (one empty pannier on the saddle). Hesk's satchel: the log (the rule, the note, the second pole, the birthday line), the old volume, the notebook, the letters, the broker's posting, Pike's lines on bark including the Fallow copy. The crossbow, hook and five bolts. Board purse: **a hundred and thirty-two earned; a hundred and two in hand** (six to the hold on the 102nd). Owed: the ferryman's rope; the Fallow hundred lies in Pike's box unpaid. Hesk's parcel has not come; no boat has come in from the west since the 91st.

**Pike's book and the wall.** **Eight lines**: the eighth is the protected Fallow line, entered on the 101st and sent up on bark (read aloud at the hold's board by Brom, and answered as not wrong); Pike has not said it in the crew's hearing. The wall's order after it is not known to the crew. The two HOLD slips are unresolved on the page. The broker's clerk's copy went west on the boat of the 102nd.

**Vastin.** One window: a courtesy copy, read twice, not understood, filed as such, returned unannotated.

**Hounds.** Unchanged: seven unaccounted for since the 96th. Nothing has come up to Thornwater's white stone since the ford (fifty-seven evenings on the 110th).

**Who knows what.** The mechanism: **six.** That he knew at noon on the 96th that the fragments answered inside: the six. That he has read himself and found something: the six know that there is a thing; none knows what. The Quieting, by that name: the six. The hold, Pike, the carter, the clerk and the west know that six went in and six came out. Nobody has put a verb between a made floor, an age with no ruler, and a boy.

**Open threads.** Two more readings, and a telling owed on her day. A question that she will not answer. A place that does not move. A count of kinds that wants a second floor. Two rings marked *guess* and a round that nobody can place. "One at a time, for money." A stake numbered thirty-one. A report eight days gone west. An arm slung until the snow; an ankle that nobody has mended; a bow that she can shoot and cannot span. A mule that is not theirs. Three unanswered letters and a parcel on a ferry somewhere.

### Next-movement inheritance (Sol plan audit §8; also written into the next packet)
Movement 7’s packet already carries the day-111 start, Oryn gone, mule with the crew, Lira two weeks into the order, and the report west (`packets/MOVEMENT-007.md:20`). It must additionally inherit:

- **Cael:** nineteen; ten fragments; deployment thirteen; Shadow still only once; the eleven Wind leans are practice; the tenth has read only himself, once; two further self-readings on different days and the whole telling to Oryn remain owed; he may read nobody merely to test the ability; Oryn’s answer and remaining price stay withheld (`manuscript/chapter-39.md:245`; `chapter-40.md:189–211,319–385`; `chapter-44.md:167–209`).
- **Bodies:** Lira’s left arm remains slung and may hold nothing until first snow, although all eight marks have taken; right tibia may run; right ankle remains taped/unmended and her left arm cannot span the bow. Brom’s hand is whole. Karis’s voice is whole but she must not lecture for a week. Cael’s calf is closed and forearm yellow-marked. Seln is unhurt (`manuscript/chapter-42.md:103–121,129–201`; `chapter-43.md:125–159`).
- **Equipment and money:** 102 marks in hand; three boxes and Oryn’s mule; Seln’s shut case; Oryn’s empty pannier left on the saddle while she carries the other and the spare rope; the yard crossbow, belt hook and five bolts travel with Brom; the ring sheets, bark slips, four chips, calfskin rings and charcoal round remain in Karis’s boxes (`manuscript/chapter-40.md:55–67,379`; `chapter-41.md:3–39`; `chapter-42.md:205–277`; `chapter-43.md:171–215`; `chapter-44.md:209–245`).
- **Knowledge:** all six know Cael functioned inside the Ring and know that his self-reading found something, but none except Cael knows what; Oryn knows the mechanism but not that Seln supplied Shadow; Karis’s hypothesis is neither spoken nor entered; the Quieting name and five findings are shared; nobody has put a verb between the facts (`manuscript/chapter-44.md:11–105,113–117`).
- **Research state:** Karis’s kinds-count is covered and unsaid; finding four says only “five or six”; stake thirty-two remains Lira’s guess; the four wheres include two map rings marked *guess* and Seln’s unplaced childhood round (`manuscript/chapter-41.md:125–159`; `chapter-43.md:3–53`; `chapter-44.md:71–95`).
- **Open accounts:** Pike has eight completed lines but the crew has not heard the eighth read at the board; the Fallow hundred remains in Pike’s box, the ferryman’s rope and two HOLD slips remain unresolved, Hesk’s parcel has not arrived, three letters are unanswered, and seven hounds remain untracked (`manuscript/chapter-40.md:221–263,383`; `AUTHOR-REPORT.md:173–179`).
- **Oryn:** day 112 closes just short of the mill; she is sixteen late out of Thornwater and seventeen late at Oxhollow, has left the mule, and has not forgiven Cael or received the self-reading (`manuscript/chapter-44.md:223–301`). Her exact location on day 113 is Millrace under M7, subject to the protected north conflict (`packets/MOVEMENT-007.md:30`).

## AFTER MOVEMENT 7 — chapters 45–52, days 111–115 past the Line (drafted 2026-10-11, author claude-opus-5-5, NO-SOURCE method)

### Coordinator rulings (binding)
- **Deployment since the Line: TWENTY-THREE.** Fourteen is Tide on Oryn on the 113th (one sitting, short count then a minute of the long, counted as one). Fifteen to twenty-three are on the 114th: Anchor, looking, at the two stakes (dawn); then at dusk Shadow (once); the read, in the ground; Anchor, looking, for the knot (absent at fourteen feet, faint at twelve, sure at ten); Anchor, the unbinding, palm on the knot (no reach, rate or price assigned); Wind, one burst; Storm, one lane up the old rut; Compression at contact; Ember, one point on the knife. Nothing was used inside the line in sight of the rim; Tide was used on nobody inside any line.
- **Shadow has now been used exactly TWICE and is closed for the rest of the book.** The seal was satisfied on the page: Cael a yard below the floor; Lira on the thirty-fourth step; Seln's back turned; the other three round the door's corner; the rim's six 260 yards off with no line below the hole's lip. Oryn does not know Seln is its source. What the rim has: a fence that failed at a knot set on the edge, and a boy who stood up and DECLARED outside. Teague saw the declaration from the ridge and asked nothing.
- **Tide and Oryn's condition.** At Millrace's bench on the 113th Cael asked leave to set his hand on a BOY'S wrist while Oryn held it, to learn what came to her hand, and was refused ("You asked. That's the rule and you kept it. The answer's no. Next."): the boy's leave was never his to ask from her, and the boy's treatment was not his lesson. On the road, an hour south of the fork, she opened it herself and he asked to read HER; she granted that, a different question, in her own words ("Yes. That's the answer, and I'm the one giving it… the same day as the lean-to and a different question, and… the rule held both times."). [Corrected at close from the Sol recheck: an earlier version of this bullet said she granted the same question.] "Self first" is honoured: he had read himself once (day 102) before reading her by leave. **The debt is still OWED:** two more self-readings on different days, then all of it to her at once; her day is hers; she refused a hurried second ("Not tonight"); nothing is forgiven.
- **The rim:** six, faceless — no seals, names or office; four crossbows and two without, the second Anchor hand one of the six (not the Ostrand-road woman: the same craft, a different weight in the laying). They waited forty paces outside the edge. Their bows are left disabled; their bolts are too stout for Oryn's bow. The crew's five bolts stayed five. The Stair slip's three hundred was taken and never paid. The second paper was lodged sealed with the first.
- **Brom took a bolt** on the 114th (high; missed the lung and the big vessel; "the bolt's the plug"); Oryn drew it and closed it when her hands worked again, outside the line. Exact states are in the author's section below, as corrected after repair.
- **The Stair's chamber:** walls cut floor to roof in five courses, each as tall as a forearm; a line that is not on the Ring stands BETWEEN the courses, on its own, recurring "every ten cords and a piece. Two hundred and twenty yards and a stride" — the Fallow's radius. (There is no continuous groove.) Karis paced the perimeter on the morning of the 115th and got two hundred. The open kit with its contents in taking-out order was found and is carried by Brom; **the case is untouched; the index and the stroke-and-bar are not decoded; nobody theorizes.** "Compact" is not said in this movement.
- **Oryn** was at Millrace on the 113th (her circuit brought her round; EXC-B7-003), crossed for a wounded patient on the 114th and went down under a held rim on the 115th; she is a stop on her route — never "sixth", never "join". **Teague** forfeited a slip to come, held the rim unasked on the 115th ("We'll hold it till you're up." / "Noted"); his crew is unnamed; the healer of no Path has not appeared.
- **#50:** Seln's "Six years I've wanted a face" stands verbatim and unglossed; the case's twelve years appears only in his window. **#51:** "Tomorrow makes three" is not explained. **"The still place"** is not said.
- **Length:** the reads split; the coordinator ruled that the Stair's action is not cut for the map's 40,000 line, and repetition is removed instead. **Names:** none minted.

### Review record
All reads on Sol (gpt-5.6-sol): editorial, cold read, source-distance (3 tracked passages, all in ch52; one dense scene), plan / canon / protected-text audit (ten line fixes applied by the coordinator, including the five protected units the author had left as markers and the replacement of the invented groove staging). Same-author repair r1 in progress when this block was written; the CLOSED note follows when the recheck returns.

### New canon minted (author's list)
- **The ground at the Stair (flag; one picture for chapters 48–52).** A ridge runs north and south; the Stair road comes south along its top and drops off its western face. Halfway down, the slope eases into a brow, and there the carters' live ruts turn north and go the whole way round a shallow bowl some five hundred yards across. The edge of the Quieting lies forty paces inside the ruts. The old straight road, two grown-over ruts, runs on down from the brow, crosses the edge between two iron bars eight feet apart (the old one on the north side, the newer on the south, with Karis's stone between), and goes to the floor: a hundred and ninety yards of grass, then thirty of floor to the hole. The floor is a bare round plate sixty of Cael's strides across, with no upright thing on it. The hole is two strides square, at the middle; the first step is on its eastern side and the stair goes down westward, forty steps, cut and not laid, every edge unworn; the air changes at the twentieth. The chamber opens westward from the stair's foot: ten strides each way; its roof is the underside of the floor, a foot above Brom's head; dust "as thin as the bloom on a plum". The rim (the ruts) is two hundred and sixty yards from the hole and sixteen hundred paces and a little more round.
- **The chamber (flag; changed in repair r1):** ten strides square, as paced on the 115th; the roof is the underside of the floor above, without beam or crack, and stands **a hand above Brom's head** (Cael had called it a foot on the 114th, at a crouch; Brom's palm lies on it with the elbow bent). Dust as thin as the bloom on a plum. **The walls** are cut from floor to roof in five courses, each as tall as a forearm, in the same hand as the bands on the Fallow's stones (Karis can see the bars). **There is no groove.** What Karis keeps for the last of the oil is the protected finding: a line that is not on the Ring, on every wall, not in the courses but *between* them, on its own, recurring "every ten cords and a piece. Two hundred and twenty yards and a stride, to the stride… at the perimeter's radius." How that spacing is read off walls ten strides long is not glossed on the page (repair flag 2). She samples the walls on twenty-nine sheets, and twice walks the foot of a wall with the short cord, counting.
- **The stair's air (changed in repair r1):** from the twentieth step down the air does not move; a lamp flame stands "like a nail" (Brom sees it in Karis's lamp before anybody names the step; Seln: "The air stops there."). In the chamber it is colder than the frost above and "smells of nothing" (Oryn). The gate bow's eighth ranging bolt lies on the thirty-first step until Lira takes it up on the 115th.
- **Hound sign at the Stair (flag):** old runs in the grass on the north side, unused since the frosts began; no den that Seln can find from outside. He says that he does not know why and will not guess. No stillhound appears in this movement.
- **The six on the rim (flag).** Hooded, in road grey, each with a carter's dust-cloth across the face; no word, cry or call from any of them at any point. Four stations outside the edge: at the gate (the brow), *the gate bow* and, two yards south of him, *the empty-handed one*; on the south, *the south bow* and *the standing one*, who carries nothing and gives one sign at the end (an arm raised and let fall); *the west bow*, alone, who never looses; *the north bow*, alone. **Four crossbows among six people.** They lay up off the ruts until the five were over the stone (Seln walked the ruts at dusk and before light and found nothing on them).
- **The second Anchor hand (flag):** the empty-handed one at the gate. The course is three lines, each as thick as a wrist, at the knee, the chest and over a tall man's head, thrown from forty paces across the old road from the old stake to the newer and on round the edge: "the same trade in a different hand… this hand threw", where the woman on the Ostrand road threaded nineteen fine points from fifteen feet. He keeps his hands inside his sleeves through the day. At the end Cael sees a pair of hands: a man's, broad, short in the finger, with a pale band where a ring was taken off before the work. Nothing else of him.
- **The knot (flag):** a single point as big as a fist, a hand above the turf, on the outer face of the **old** stake; three courses come into it from the south and three from the north (the two ends of one ring), and nothing comes out. Cael first looks for it on the newer stake, where the lines landed: "A rope is not made fast where it lands. It is made fast where the hauling starts." He lies in the north rut, reaches round the bar and lays his palm on it. How far round the edge the course ran is not seen; he feels "a great deal of it" run out.
- **The rim's bolts (flag): thirteen.** Gate bow eleven (Brom's shoulder; one during the run; eight ranging shots at the hole, the eighth down the stair to the thirty-first step; one at Cael outside the stone at dusk, which misses); north bow one and south bow one, lofted during the run; west bow none. They are too stout for the channel of the healer's bow (Brom tries one along the stock). On the 115th the boy lays the recovered shafts in a row by the four bows, Lira adds the one from the stair, and the head cut from Brom's bolt lies by the stone; the figure thirteen is given once, in the night log of the 114th. Teague reads eleven stirrup-prints "and a twelfth half made" on the brow.
- **How the six leave (flag):** each bow has its string cut and the nut drawn from its lock, and is laid down on its side; the maker's stamp has been filed off the stock and the place oiled; no armoury number. Seln: "bought for one day, by somebody who meant from the first to leave it lying." Nothing else is left at any station. The four bows lie in a row by the stone on the 115th, one in two pieces.
- **The boxes were not touched** by the six: Seln had laid a stem of grass under every knot. His reason: six people with a quarter of a mile of rim apiece do not leave it.
- **Karis's leaf:** on the night of the 113th she takes the leaf of the trials (with the struck sixth row) out of the middle box and carries it inside her coat.
- **The second paper's history (flag):** written at one desk and one sitting with the first (the same pricked margin, from a wheel with a tooth missing, so that every eleventh hole stands wide; the same fault in a letter's foot); sealed inside the same wrapper, with three hundred counted in a bag and a word of direction that it was not to be posted until the first was answered; kept in the broker's strong-box, he not knowing what it was; brought up by the clerk on the morning after the Fallow line was said; said twice to the meadow, and nobody scratched; read by Teague, who hung it on the nail himself, eight days before the 112th. It carries the hook and the bar.
- **The nail (flag):** a long nail in the plaster by the lower corner of the wall-sheet, where a slip is hung that a captain has taken down, read through and handed back, "so that the next captain knows it has been weighed and by whom". There is hardly ever anything on it.
- **The wall on the 112th:** the five are eleventh; the crew of four with the wooden foot is over them again. Pike: "The cloth can't say whose fault a thing was. It can only say what was on the cart."
- **Pike's HOLD on his own mark:** "If they're late down, nobody comes to me for that slip. It stays theirs till I've seen them or seen where they stopped." He has said yes to one man in thirty years.
- **Brom's father and the horse (flag):** the year Brom was nine, a grey of sixteen hands, worth three hundred, bought at a fair for a hundred from a dealer who would not say why; it went over backward on a hard road, broke his father's collarbone and a groom's leg. "The dealer had priced that horse to a penny. It was my father who read the figure as luck."
- **Rooke's line, as Brom tells it:** said in his ear in his first season, when he had a second-year shut into the corner of the frames with his own back to the gap.
- **Seln's window:** the clerk is no longer young (his capitals are of a pattern not taught for twenty years); there were two people in that room, a hand that wrote and a voice that knew the ground. He weighs walking south alone with the case before light, and does not.
- **Millrace (flag):** an hour below the fork, in a narrow green valley; fast brown water over a weir of stakes and stone; a race along the bank to a tall grey mill with a wheel as high as a barn door; a fence of split posts and a dozen roofs; a ford below, with a flat stone on each bank; four chimneys. The ford-keeper is a woman who sits on the nearer stone with a net across her knees and counts people off the ridge. The healer's place is a lean-to of three board walls and a turf roof against the mill's southern wall, with a plank on two kegs and a bench along the wall. The mill's carter is a round floured man; a second carter, long and stooped, takes her word north.
- **The fifteen:** a miller's man whose right hand went between the hoist's rope and its drum nine days before; a girl of three or four with a rattling chest, low and on the one side (not the child of the far gate); an old woman, small and upright, carried down the valley on a hurdle by two grandsons, who went through the fourth rung of a ladder getting a ham down ("It's got a ladder in it."); and a dozen with one hand each. Three mendings: "That's the day."
- **Her sum on the 113th (flag):** due at Millrace on the 96th, in on the 112th: sixteen. Seventeen at the ford with the oxen, on the 121st. A half-day up, a day, a half-day down: nineteen at the ford, and the child not heard before the 128th. **Her word north:** late, nineteen "or more", at the edge of bad ground a half-day south of the mill; if the child's breathing changes, send down the water to the mill, and the mill sends up the ridge.
- **Lira's bowls (flag):** "We lay six bowls… Brom puts six out every night and takes one back." / "I'll eat out of it. I'll not sit in a place." At the Stair she eats out of the last of the six, standing.
- **The mill's jenny (flag):** mouse-grey, enormous in the belly, ears like oars; she leads "as a gate swings", comes up the notch backward, and brays at every dusk. The miller will thank Oryn to take her "as far as the snow". Oryn does not touch the brown mule.
- **The tenth on another person (flag; changed in repair r1).** He asks twice on the 113th, and they are two questions: at Millrace he asks the healer, at her own bench, with her line waiting, and is refused; on the road she offers, as "a woman on a rock" who can say yes (her protected line: "the same day as the lean-to and a different question"). **The short reading is done with one hand**, his right on her wrist (inside upward, broad and brown, a white scar across the heel of the hand): the going-out leaves that hand and is back under his palm almost before he has counted; at three there are banks, "close… and high", and by ten he knows the Path "as a friend's step on a stair" is known. For the minute she has him put his left hand on the back of her neck as well: one channel, one thing in it, which does not wander or pool, and leans to meet whatever touches it. Bill to him: the reading hand slow for an hour, and hunger.
- **Wind asked on the stillness:** he does not load on the third count; he asks on "four, and", when the bowman has gone entirely still (the gate-walk's "Shooting's stopping", by way of Lira).
- **Injuries, as fixed on the page.** Brom: a bolt through the back of the right shoulder, a hand below its top, the head out under the collar-bone by a thumb's length; taped round the shaft in the chamber with what was left of Lira's ankle tape; outside the stone Seln cuts the shaft below the head and Oryn draws it; mended in an hour; the arm bound across his chest on the 115th; nothing on that side "till I've said so". Lira: her ankle goes at about the twentieth step and the slung arm takes the wall; the lowest mark of each row opens; bound with a strip of Cael's shirt; closed in a quarter of an hour at the stone; the sling tied shorter, the hand at the collar-bone. Cael: both hands and forearms cold and dead for the evening (Shadow); the right forearm struck (it bends by morning); a weal across the left palm, wound in tape, not mended. Oryn: three mendings on the 113th; two on the night of the 114th ("There isn't a third in me tonight").
- **The 115th.** Teague's four on the rim: Teague on the brow, the spear south, the boy west, the bow north; Teague lends his horn lantern. The pacing runs from the stone northward with the Quieting on the right hand; Lira carries the cord's far end and finds the edge with her foot; Seln cuts the turfs; the old stakes stand within a boot of them; the round is the Fallow's, cord for cord, with the same odd strides.
- **The kit (flag; order changed in repair r1):** found in the middle of the chamber floor. A cord knotted at every fifth span lies run out straight toward the eastern wall for half the room, with the rest of it in a coil that was never paid out ("measuring from the middle of that room to its wall, and had got half way"); then a mallet with an iron-bound head; a bar numbered thirty-two with the stroke and the bar, clean of rust; and a field ledger on its face, last and nearest. Behind them a flat case of board under tarred canvas, as long as a man's arm, with a shoulder strap, its lid thrown back and nothing in it. Karis: "a kit for one thing… He came to put iron on an edge." Seln: "They lie in the order that they came out… Nobody packs so. He meant to take them up again." One track in the dust came to it from the door; Seln cannot find it going out.
- **The ledger (flag; changed in repair r1):** limp hide, no bigger than Hesk's book, ruled in faint blue, a page and a half written in a small upright hand, standing, on a knee. A head line of four signs from an index that Seln does not know, and the number; eight distances by the compass points and half-points, "to floor", every one the same; a line headed *Reach, edge to centre.*, gone over twice (its figure is read aloud to Karis and not printed); a few lines on the floor and the stair, not read out; and on the second page the last line and a stroke of the pencil going off toward the edge. **Its eight stations are the old irons:** Karis found an old scaled bar at each of those eight points that morning, within a boot of her turfs. Seln therefore dates the book with the old iron at the stone, **five cold terms**, which is what lets the closing log's "five years ago" stand. **The stroke-and-bar is two visits:** it lay on the unused bar in this man's case five cold terms ago, and somebody set the same mark on the newer bar at the stone "a good deal later"; Seln will put neither a meaning nor a date on it. Seln puts the ledger inside his own coat.
- **Karis's count:** the Ring's figure had had her thumb over it for twelve days; "the cup was under it" and "the cup and the bowl together were over it". The hook with two bars is "close" on two walls, so wide was only the ninth stone. Forty-one at both, "in the courses here", with no forty-second and none wanting; the line between the courses stands outside that count. She had been over the Fallow's eleven strips for four days at a trestle in a barn.
- **The brass on the hide:** she strikes out *guess* beside the ring a day south of Lowmarch, and says that she is going to find out how far wrong a map of days can be along one line.
- **Teague (flag):** the slip is a shingle, nine days on the oak: *Millrace. Three stakes of the weir, gone in the last high water. To be set before the ground shuts. Four marks.* He has a day's posts still to set on the forty-mark fence. He tells his three the true reason at the fork and gives them leave to stay; none does. He sets two stakes and leaves the third, which forfeits the four marks. He once walked eleven cold terms beside a Wind, in another crew, and has worked beside two Embers. His unreported rank was earned on a ledge in the cold.
- **Roles (all unnamed):** the mill's carter; the stooped carter; the ford-keeper at Millrace; the miller and his wife; the miller's man; the father and the small girl; the old woman and her two grandsons; a carter with a wrenched back, a boy with a stye, a woman with a tooth, a sullen man with a brother; the girl at the next boards; the six on the rim.

### State at movement end (author; governed by the rulings above and by the surviving prose)
**Calendar.** Late afternoon of the **hundred and fifteenth day past the Line**, at the stone on the eastern edge of the Long Stair, in full sun. No season, month or English weekday name is on the page ("the cold term", "frost", "the thaw", "the snow", "a dry month"). Days on the page: 111, 112, 113, 114, 115; in Seln's window, the night of the 112th; in Teague's, the 113th, 114th and the dawn of the 115th. Weather: hard frost every night, ringing roads, ice along Lowmarch's bank; no snow. The next movement opens where this one stops.

**Where everyone is.** The six at the stone, with two mules (the brown, the crew's; the mill's grey jenny, Oryn's for now), three boxes, and the kit on the turf. Teague and his three have gone back up the ridge toward Millrace. The six from the rim went off north, south and west at dusk on the 114th and have not been seen since.

**Cael.** Nineteen. **Ten confirmed fragments.** **Deployment since the Line: twenty-three**, by his own log (fourteen on the 113th; nine on the 114th; none on the 115th). **Shadow used twice in all** (the Ring; the Stair), unseen by anybody outside the circle who could carry it; it is shut again. Anchor: looked twice and unbound once. The tenth has now read one other person, with her leave, at one sitting; **he has still read himself once only**, and owes Oryn two more readings on different days and then all of it; she told him "not tonight" on the 113th. What he found in himself is unsaid and unnamed aloud. Body: the right forearm struck (bends on the 115th); a weal across the left palm, taped, unmended; hands warm again. He has said *Noted* twice to Teague. He holds the kit (Brom carries it). Hesk's satchel: the log, the old volume, the notebook, the letters, both of the broker's papers, Pike's lines on bark. Three letters still unanswered.

**Lira.** Wind; Iron, Rank One. No burst. **Left arm: two marks reopened on the stair wall on the 114th, closed again that night; slung shorter, the hand at the collar-bone; under the order until first snow, and "not going to hold anything else this season".** Right ankle taped, unmended, gone once more on the stair; her tape is used up. She ran two hundred and twenty yards on the released shin. She carried the healer's bow down and held the stair with it for a day without loosing. She found sixty-two stations of an edge with her foot. Her "walk away" is entered, and she went.

**Brom.** Iron Skin; Copper. **Right shoulder: bolted through from behind on the 114th, mended once that night; the arm bound across his chest; nothing on that side until Oryn says.** He carried the kit up forty steps on the left. He has the healer's bow again, its hook, and **five bolts**. He has quoted Rooke. He lays six bowls.

**Karis.** Ember; Iron, Rank Three. One point set, on her lamp, outside the stone on the night of the 114th. Holds: **forty sheets** (eleven of the Ring, twenty-nine of the Stair); **forty-one kinds, the same at both**, entered and underlined; **the line between the courses** (not on the Ring; on every wall of the chamber; recurring at ten cords and a piece, two hundred and twenty yards and a stride, the perimeter's radius), shown on the eastern wall's sheets; the Stair's perimeter table (the Fallow's round, to the stride); the surveyor's *Reach, edge to centre* figure, written down from Seln's reading; the leaf of the trials, inside her coat; the calfskin, with *guess* struck out beside the Stair's ring and still standing beside the far south-eastern one; the Ternhall brass, out of its cloth and held over the hide. Her hypothesis is unentered and unsaid. She has said that the floor was carved by somebody who knew the circle's size, and nobody took it up.

**Seln.** Shadow; Bronze. Unmarked. Angry once: a stock broken across the stone, and "No faces." **The case is on its strap, on him, shut. The kit's ledger is inside his coat.** He has said that he wants a face. He has read the lure, the trap and the kit. He has dated the ledger with the old irons (five cold terms) and has said that the stroke-and-bar stands for two separate visits, the later one undated; he has refused to name the index or the mark, or to say what became of the surveyor.

**Oryn.** Tide; Iron, Rank Four. **On the round's terms, as a stop:** "when I say lie still, you lie still, and when I say I can't help you, you believe me"; she goes when the round cannot spare her. Nineteen days late at the ford with the oxen by her own sum, "or more"; word sent north by a carter, with where to send for the child. Five mendings in two days (three at Millrace; Brom and Lira at the stone). She refused him at her own bench and offered on the road, the same day ("a different question"). She has been read by him once, short (one hand on her wrist) and long, and has been told what he found in her. She has not forgiven him, and has not been told what he found in himself. She does not know where the ninth came from. Her pannier and rope are on the grey jenny.

**Teague.** He stood for the five at Lowmarch and sat at "eleventh". He hung the second paper on the nail. He took and forfeited a four-mark slip to be near; he saw one boy, outside the edge, declare a Wind, a fire and a third thing that he cannot name; he said only that he saw a man walk out of the Quiet declaring, and asked nothing. His three walked the ruts by lantern and brought in three crossbows. He held the rim through the 115th and lent a lantern. He still has a day's posts on the forty-mark fence. His crew is unnamed; the healer of no Path has not appeared.

**The six.** Unseen since dusk on the 114th. No face, name, seal, office or word. They know that a fence failed at its knot on the edge, and that a boy came out of the old road and declared, outside, more than one thing. They saw nothing work inside. They did not touch the boxes.

**Objects and money.** Board purse: **a hundred and thirty-two earned; a hundred and two in hand**, unchanged. The three hundred lies in Pike's box against the broker's mark, and will not be marked. The Fallow hundred lies there too. **The kit:** case, pacing cord knotted at the fives, stake thirty-two with the stroke and the bar and a bright turned point, mallet; the ledger apart, with Seln. Four crossbows by the stone, strings cut and locks empty, one broken; the rim's recovered shafts in a row beside them (thirteen loosed, by the log of the 114th), useless to the healer's bow. Karis's lamp filled; Teague's lantern returned. Owed still: the ferryman's rope; three replies; Hesk's parcel has not come.

**Pike's book and the wall.** **Eight lines**; the eighth said twice in the crew's hearing on the 112th. **Eleventh; Teague ten up.** The Stair paper carries five strokes and **HOLD, on Pike's own mark**, said to the room and to be said to the meadow. Pike has said that his book has no column for what the eighth line means.

**Who knows what.** The mechanism: six. That the fragments answered at the Stair: the six. Which fragments, by Path: the six; where the ninth came from: the five. Teague and his three: a declaration outside the edge, of more than one kind. Pike and the room: that six went in at the Fallow and six came out. The registry's side: as above. Nobody has said why anything answers him inside. Nobody has put a verb between a made floor, an age with no ruler, the same round twice, a circle whose size the carvers knew, and a boy.

**Open threads.** A bearing through two rings on a calfskin, and a third thing on it. A line between the courses of a wall that keeps a circle's radius, and that the Ring does not carry. A mark set twice, at two visits, that nobody will date. A surveyor who stopped in the middle of a line. An index and a two-stroke mark that Seln will not read. Six people who went off a rim without their bows. Two readings and a telling, owed. A healer nineteen late and counting, with a child whom she cannot hear. An arm that held a stair; an ankle that nobody has mended; a shoulder mended once. A captain who saw three things and asked nothing. A paper with HOLD on it that will never be paid. A fence at forty with a day's posts to set.

### Next-movement inheritance (Sol plan audit §8; also written into the next packet)
Movement 8's packet already carries the day-115 rim, bearing, two mules, six people, Brom mended once, Lira closed again and Teague turning back (`MOVEMENT-008.md:18–20`). Its author must additionally inherit these surviving-prose facts:

- **Cael and the debt:** nineteen; ten fragments; deployment twenty-three; Shadow has now been used exactly twice and is closed for the rest of the book; Tide has read Oryn once by leave but Cael has still read himself only once. Two self-readings on different days and the full telling remain owed, Oryn's day remains hers, and neither her withheld answer nor forgiveness has been supplied (48:229–235,273–279; 51:197–225; 52:355–359).
- **Bills and bodies:** Cael's right forearm bends but carries Compression's blow, and his left palm is taped with an unmended weal; Lira's reopened marks are shut and the sling is shorter, but the no-hold order and taped/unmended ankle stand; Brom's right arm is bound after one shoulder mending and must not be used until Oryn says (51:123–163; 52:5,75).
- **Equipment:** Brom has Oryn's bow, hook and five unshot bolts; the four captured crossbows are disabled and their thirteen bolts do not fit it. There are three archive boxes, the crew's brown mule, Oryn's grey jenny, 102 marks, the recovered kit, and Seln's shut case (51:167–197; 52:303–359).
- **Research custody:** Seln has the field ledger inside his coat; Karis has eleven Ring sheets plus twenty-nine Stair sheets, forty-one kinds, the trials leaf, the perimeter table, the calfskin with the Stair's *guess* struck and the south-eastern *guess* still standing, and the Ternhall straightedge (52:165–209,247–305,355–369).
- **Knowledge limits:** the index, stroke-and-bar and last unfinished entry remain uninterpreted; Karis's hypothesis remains unspoken and unentered; nobody has theorized why Cael works inside; Oryn still does not know Seln supplied Shadow; Teague knows only that one practitioner walked out declaring (50:181–213; 51:239–257; 52:169–213).
- **Open accounts:** the three hundred is taken and never to be paid; Pike's Fallow hundred, the ferryman's rope, Hesk's parcel and three replies remain outstanding; Teague forfeited the four-mark weir job and still has a day's fence posts (51:263–325; 52:355–359).
- **Oryn's route:** she leaves Movement 7 nineteen days late at the oxen ford, “or more,” with word sent north about where she is and where to send if the child's breathing changes; she remains a stop on her route, never a sixth or a joiner (47:133–229; 48:273–279).
- **Other people:** the six faceless attackers are gone with no attributable identity; Teague's three and every member of the rim remain unnamed; the healer of no Path has not appeared (51:91–103,263–383).

**Movement 7 CLOSED (2026-10-11).** Same-author repair r1: the ten audit fixes kept and their joins smoothed; ch52's three tracked passages restaged (scene 2 re-entered on the bolt at the thirty-first step; the kit read from the cord inward); the design confirmed once; twice-told states removed from ch49–51 with the night log whole; the closing log's "five years ago" made to stand (the ledger's eight stations are the old irons, five cold terms by Seln; the stroke-and-bar is "two visits", the later undated; nothing decoded); a register pass. Sol recheck r1: **CLOSE WITH LINE FIXES** — seven applied by the coordinator: **what Cael asked at Millrace's bench is corrected to the source's actual distinction** (leave to touch a boy's wrist while Oryn held it — refused; on the road he asks to read HER — granted, "a different question"), with the bench's count and the day's log to match; and ch52 says Karis reads the radius figure from scaled divisions within the lone line, not as a spacing along the wall. All three tracked passages RESOLVED, no new tracking. Gates 0; overlap as below. 42413 words (the length ruled by the coordinator: the Stair's action not cut for the map's 40,000 line). Author-sourced sections above refreshed from the post-repair report; where they still describe the bench request as a request to read Oryn, the Tide ruling above governs.

**Movement 7, post-close correction (2026-10-11).** One of the recheck's own line fixes (ch47, "For the first time in his life, he…") produced an 8-word run shared with the source, which the overlap gate caught after the first publish. The coordinator recomposed the sentence ("He had never before asked leave to put his hand on another person. He had asked, and had been told that he might not…") and republished. Overlap now 0 unprotected.
